{"parentUuid":"c06ade82-0058-4826-926d-5fb13737b49d","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a5d6827d8ff2888aa","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use when the user wants visual explanations, diagrams, or illustrations in the hand-drawn excalidraw aesthetic and asks for the codex path. Codex's bundled image_gen tool runs through the logged-in account, so no GEMINI_API_KEY is required and it's often faster than the Gemini route. This is the RECOMMENDED DEFAULT path for excalidraw image generation: it needs no API key and runs through the logged-in Codex account. Default to codex whenever the user does not explicitly ask for the Gemini route.\n- excalidraw-codex-simple\n- excalidraw-deck: Build hand-drawn, Excalidraw-style HTML slide decks that explain a concept through sketch-aesthetic vector diagrams — rough.js strokes on a dark canvas, full-bleed, one diagram per slide with a caption underneath, arrow-key and tap navigation. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Use after picking favorites from excalidraw-gen batches. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Also triggers on \"check post engagement\", \"update post metrics\", or reviewing social media performance. Use when the user wants to write a newsletter issue, draft an email to their list, write a LifeNotes-style essay, or asks for help with newsletter writing. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. Also trigger when the user references a specific tengu_ flag name, or the existing \"GrowthBook Flag Enabler\" Proxyman rule, or wants to add another flag to it. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. $0.001; xAI Grok STT batch is $0.10/hour = 1.6667 credits/min.\nconst CREDITS_PER_MINUTE = 1.67;\n\nexport const customerRouter = createTRPCRouter({\n  /**\n   * Get all licenses with their individual 0) {\n      return {\n        licenses: [],\n        creditsPerMinute: CREDITS_PER_MINUTE,\n        totalCredits: 0,\n        totalMinutesRemaining: 0,\n      };\n    }\n\n    // Credits are pooled per account. would\n    // double-count a multi-key account's pooled balance).\n    const userIds = Array.from(new Set(licenses.map((l) => l.userId)));\n    const balanceMap = await getCreditBalancesForUsers(userIds);\n\n    const licensesWithCredits = licenses.map((license) => {\n      const credits = balanceMap.get(license.userId) || 0;\n      return {\n        id: license.id,\n        key: license.key,\n        status: license.status,\n        credits,\n        minutesRemaining: Math.floor(credits / CREDITS_PER_MINUTE),\n        createdAt: license.createdAt.toISOString(),\n        stripeCustomerId: license.stripeCustomerId,\n        polarCustomerId: license.polarCustomerId,\n      };\n    });\n\n    const totalCredits = userIds.reduce(\n      (sum, uid) => sum + (balanceMap.get(uid) || 0),\n      0\n    );\n\n    return {\n      licenses: licensesWithCredits,\n      creditsPerMinute: CREDITS_PER_MINUTE,\n      totalCredits,\n      totalMinutesRemaining: Math.floor(totalCredits / CREDITS_PER_MINUTE),\n    };\n  }),\n\n  /**\n   * Get credit balance for the authenticated user.\n   */\n  credits: protectedProcedure.query(async ({ ctx }) => {\n    const userEmail = ctx.user.email?.toLowerCase();\n\n    if (!userEmail) {\n      throw new TRPCError({\n        code: \"BAD_REQUEST\",\n        message: \"User email not found\",\n      });\n    }\n\n    const licenses = await getAccountKeysByEmail(userEmail);\n\n    if (licenses.length === 0) {\n      return {\n        totalCredits: 0,\n        minutesRemaining: 0,\n        creditsPerMinute: CREDITS_PER_MINUTE,\n      };\n    }\n\n    // Credits are pooled per account: sum the DISTINCT user balances so a\n    // multi-key account's pooled balance isn't counted once per key.\n    const userIds = Array.from(new Set(licenses.map((l) => l.userId)));\n    const balanceMap = await getCreditBalancesForUsers(userIds);\n\n    let totalCredits = 0;\n    for (const uid of userIds) {\n      totalCredits += balanceMap.get(uid) || 0;\n    }\n\n    const minutesRemaining = Math.floor(totalCredits / CREDITS_PER_MINUTE);\n\n    return {\n      totalCredits,\n      minutesRemaining,\n      creditsPerMinute: CREDITS_PER_MINUTE,\n    };\n  }),\n\n  /**\n   * Paid credit-pack top-up history for the authenticated user.\n   *\n   * Returns the user's PAID credit grants (source_type = 'stripe_credit_pack')\n   * newest-first, including expired ones so the dashboard can show their status.\n   * Free/included bundles and admin/legacy grants are intentionally excluded.\n   */\n  creditHistory: protectedProcedure.query(async ({ ctx }) => {\n    const userEmail = ctx.user.email?.toLowerCase();\n\n    if (!userEmail) {\n      throw new TRPCError({\n        code: \"BAD_REQUEST\",\n        message: \"User email not found\",\n      });\n    }\n\n    const licenses = await getAccountKeysByEmail(userEmail);\n    if (licenses.length === 0) {\n      return { grants: [] };\n    }\n\n    // Credits are pooled per account, so history is keyed by the licenses'\n    // distinct owning users (de-duped) rather than per license.\n    const grants = await getPaidCreditGrantsForUsers(\n      Array.from(new Set(licenses.map((l) => l.userId)))\n    );\n\n    const now = Date.now();\n    return {\n      grants: grants.map((g) => ({\n        id: g.id,\n        createdAt: g.createdAt.toISOString(),\n        expiresAt: g.expiresAt ? g.expiresAt.toISOString() : null,\n        originalAmount: g.originalAmount,\n        remainingAmount: g.remainingAmount,\n        // Surface expiry as a derived flag so the client doesn't re-parse dates;\n        // a grant is expired once its expiresAt has passed, regardless of the\n        // stored status (enforcement is a lazy filter, not a cron).\n        expired: g.expiresAt ? g.expiresAt.getTime() <= now : false,\n        status: g.status,\n      })),\n    };\n  }),\n\n  /**\n   * Check which billing providers the user has history with.\n   * Queries license_keys to determine billing provider history.\n   */\n  billingProviders: protectedProcedure.query(async ({ ctx }) => {\n    const userEmail = ctx.user.email?.toLowerCase();\n    if (!userEmail) return { hasStripe: false, hasPolar: false };\n    const licenses = await getAccountKeysByEmail(userEmail);\n    return {\n      hasStripe: licenses.some((l) => !!l.stripeCustomerId),\n      hasPolar: licenses.some((l) => !!l.polarCustomerId),\n    };\n  }),\n\n  /**\n   * Generate Stripe billing portal session URL.\n   */\n  stripePortalUrl: protectedProcedure.mutation(async ({ ctx }) => {\n    const userEmail = ctx.user.email?.toLowerCase();\n\n    if (!userEmail) {\n      throw new TRPCError({\n        code: {"parentUuid":"0634ee42-17f5-4722-a48e-77c2f4483138","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77ec06fb-aafc-4d6b-b92c-3296ca279672","agentId":"a5d6827d8ff2888aa","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0133vgKW2QrGQJvfrhNE7QAQ","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * tRPC Server Configuration for App Router\n3\t *\n4\t * This file sets up:\n5\t * 1. Context creation with Better Auth\n6\t * 2. Three procedure types: public, protected, admin\n7\t * 3. superjson transformer for date/Map/Set serialization\n8\t *\n9\t * ARCHITECTURE NOTES:\n10\t * - Uses App Router pattern with fetchRequestHandler (not Pages Router createNextApiHandler)\n11\t * - Context receives Headers object and checks Better Auth session\n12\t * - Three-tier auth: publicProcedure (anyone), protectedProcedure (logged in), adminProcedure (role === \"admin\")\n13\t */\n14\timport { initTRPC, TRPCError } from \"@trpc/server\";\n15\timport superjson from \"superjson\";\n16\t\n17\timport type { User as BetterAuthUser } from \"better-auth/types\";\n18\t\n19\timport { auth } from \"@/src/lib/auth\";\n20\t\n21\t/**\n22\t * Context passed to every tRPC procedure.\n23\t *\n24\t * @property user - The authenticated Better Auth user, or null if not logged in\n25\t * @property isAdmin - Whether the user is in the admin email whitelist\n26\t * @property headers - Request headers (for rate limiting, IP extraction, etc.)\n27\t */\n28\texport interface TRPCContext {\n29\t  user: BetterAuthUser | null;\n30\t  isAdmin: boolean;\n31\t  headers: Headers;\n32\t}\n33\t\n34\t/**\n35\t * Creates tRPC context from incoming request headers.\n36\t *\n37\t * FLOW:\n38\t * 1. Gets session from Better Auth using request headers\n39\t * 2. Checks admin status via email whitelist\n40\t * 3. Returns context object for use in all procedures\n41\t *\n42\t * @param opts - Object containing request headers (from App Router)\n43\t * @returns Promise resolving to TRPCContext\n44\t */\n45\texport async function createTRPCContext(opts: {\n46\t  headers: Headers;\n47\t}): Promise<TRPCContext> {\n48\t  const session = await auth.api.getSession({ headers: opts.headers });\n49\t  const user = session?.user ?? null;\n50\t  const isAdmin = user?.role === \"admin\";\n51\t\n52\t  return { user, isAdmin, headers: opts.headers };\n53\t}\n54\t\n55\t/**\n56\t * Initialize tRPC with context type and superjson transformer.\n57\t *\n58\t * superjson enables serialization of:\n59\t * - Date objects\n60\t * - Map/Set\n61\t * - BigInt\n62\t * - undefined (preserved, not converted to null)\n63\t */\n64\tconst t = initTRPC.context<TRPCContext>().create({\n65\t  transformer: superjson,\n66\t  errorFormatter({ shape }) {\n67\t    return shape;\n68\t  },\n69\t});\n70\t\n71\t/**\n72\t * Middleware: Ensure user is authenticated.\n73\t *\n74\t * Throws UNAUTHORIZED if no user in context.\n75\t * Narrows ctx.user type from User | null to User.\n76\t */\n77\tconst isAuthed = t.middleware(({ ctx, next }) => {\n78\t  if (!ctx.user) {\n79\t    throw new TRPCError({\n80\t      code: \"UNAUTHORIZED\",\n81\t      message: \"You must be signed in to access this resource\",\n82\t    });\n83\t  }\n84\t  return next({\n85\t    ctx: {\n86\t      ...ctx,\n87\t      user: ctx.user, // Now guaranteed non-null\n88\t    },\n89\t  });\n90\t});\n91\t\n92\t/**\n93\t * Middleware: Ensure user is an admin.\n94\t *\n95\t * CHECKS:\n96\t * 1. User must be authenticated\n97\t * 2. User must have role === \"admin\" in the database\n98\t *\n99\t * Throws UNAUTHORIZED if not logged in, FORBIDDEN if not admin.\n100\t */\n101\tconst isAdmin = t.middleware(({ ctx, next }) => {\n102\t  if (!ctx.user) {\n103\t    throw new TRPCError({\n104\t      code: \"UNAUTHORIZED\",\n105\t      message: \"You must be signed in\",\n106\t    });\n107\t  }\n108\t  if (!ctx.isAdmin) {\n109\t    throw new TRPCError({\n110\t      code: \"FORBIDDEN\",\n111\t      message: \"Admin access required\",\n112\t    });\n113\t  }\n114\t  return next({\n115\t    ctx: {\n116\t      ...ctx,\n117\t      user: ctx.user,\n118\t    },\n119\t  });\n120\t});\n121\t\n122\t// ============================================================================\n123\t// EXPORTS\n124\t// ============================================================================\n125\t\n126\t/**\n127\t * Creates a new tRPC router.\n128\t * Use this to define procedure groups (e.g., licenseRouter, adminRouter).\n129\t */\n130\texport const createTRPCRouter = t.router;\n131\t\n132\t/**\n133\t * Public procedure - no authentication required.\n134\t * Use for: license validation, checkout URLs, public downloads\n135\t */\n136\texport const publicProcedure = t.procedure;\n137\t\n138\t/**\n139\t * Protected procedure - requires authenticated user.\n140\t * Use for: user-specific operations that any logged-in user can perform\n141\t */\n142\texport const protectedProcedure = t.procedure.use(isAuthed);\n143\t\n144\t/**\n145\t * Admin procedure - requires admin email.\n146\t * Use for: dashboard stats, customer management, sync operations\n147\t */\n148\texport const adminProcedure = t.procedure.use(isAdmin);\n149\t"}]},"uuid":"073aaf7a-b0fc-4c02-a14d-5c100212e5f2","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:43:14.433Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/server/api/trpc.ts","content":"/**\n * tRPC Server Configuration for App Router\n *\n * This file sets up:\n * 1. Context creation with Better Auth\n * 2. Three procedure types: public, protected, admin\n * 3. superjson transformer for date/Map/Set serialization\n *\n * ARCHITECTURE NOTES:\n * - Uses App Router pattern with fetchRequestHandler (not Pages Router createNextApiHandler)\n * - Context receives Headers object and checks Better Auth session\n * - Three-tier auth: publicProcedure (anyone), protectedProcedure (logged in), adminProcedure (role === \"admin\")\n */\nimport { initTRPC, TRPCError } from \"@trpc/server\";\nimport superjson from \"superjson\";\n\nimport type { User as BetterAuthUser } from \"better-auth/types\";\n\nimport { auth } from \"@/src/lib/auth\";\n\n/**\n * Context passed to every tRPC procedure.\n *\n * @property user - The authenticated Better Auth user, or null if not logged in\n * @property isAdmin - Whether the user is in the admin email whitelist\n * @property headers - Request headers (for rate limiting, IP extraction, etc.)\n */\nexport interface TRPCContext {\n  user: BetterAuthUser | null;\n  isAdmin: boolean;\n  headers: Headers;\n}\n\n/**\n * Creates tRPC context from incoming request headers.\n *\n * FLOW:\n * 1. Gets session from Better Auth using request headers\n * 2. Checks admin status via email whitelist\n * 3. Returns context object for use in all procedures\n *\n * @param opts - Object containing request headers (from App Router)\n * @returns Promise resolving to TRPCContext\n */\nexport async function createTRPCContext(opts: {\n  headers: Headers;\n}): Promise<TRPCContext> {\n  const session = await auth.api.getSession({ headers: opts.headers });\n  const user = session?.user ?? null;\n  const isAdmin = user?.role === \"admin\";\n\n  return { user, isAdmin, headers: opts.headers };\n}\n\n/**\n * Initialize tRPC with context type and superjson transformer.\n *\n * superjson enables serialization of:\n * - Date objects\n * - Map/Set\n * - BigInt\n * - undefined (preserved, not converted to null)\n */\nconst t = initTRPC.context<TRPCContext>().create({\n  transformer: superjson,\n  errorFormatter({ shape }) {\n    return shape;\n  },\n});\n\n/**\n * Middleware: Ensure user is authenticated.\n *\n * Throws UNAUTHORIZED if no user in context.\n * Narrows ctx.user type from User | null to User.\n */\nconst isAuthed = t.middleware(({ ctx, next }) => {\n  if (!ctx.user) {\n    throw new TRPCError({\n      code: \"UNAUTHORIZED\",\n      message: \"You must be signed in to access this resource\",\n    });\n  }\n  return next({\n    ctx: {\n      ...ctx,\n      user: ctx.user, // Now guaranteed non-null\n    },\n  });\n});\n\n/**\n * Middleware: Ensure user is an admin.\n *\n * CHECKS:\n * 1. User must be authenticated\n * 2. User must have role === \"admin\" in the database\n *\n * Throws UNAUTHORIZED if not logged in, FORBIDDEN if not admin.\n */\nconst isAdmin = t.middleware(({ ctx, next }) => {\n  if (!ctx.user) {\n    throw new TRPCError({\n      code: \"UNAUTHORIZED\",\n      message: \"You must be signed in\",\n    });\n  }\n  if (!ctx.isAdmin) {\n    throw new TRPCError({\n      code: \"FORBIDDEN\",\n      message: \"Admin access required\",\n    });\n  }\n  return next({\n    ctx: {\n      ...ctx,\n      user: ctx.user,\n    },\n  });\n});\n\n// ============================================================================\n// EXPORTS\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n * Creates a new tRPC router.\n * Use this to define procedure groups (e.g., licenseRouter, adminRouter).\n */\nexport const createTRPCRouter = t.router;\n\n/**\n * Public procedure - no authentication required.\n * Use for: license validation, checkout URLs, public downloads\n */\nexport const publicProcedure = t.procedure;\n\n/**\n * Protected procedure - requires authenticated user.\n * Use for: user-specific operations that any logged-in user can perform\n */\nexport const protectedProcedure = t.procedure.use(isAuthed);\n\n/**\n * Admin procedure - requires admin email.\n * Use for: dashboard stats, customer management, sync operations\n */\nexport const adminProcedure = t.procedure.use(isAdmin);\n","numLines":149,"startLine":1,"totalLines":149}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0634ee42-17f5-4722-a48e-77c2f4483138","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

Resolve balances by the licenses' distinct\n39\t    // owning users; each license then reports its account balance.\n40\t    const userIds = Array.from(new Set(licenses.map((l) => l.userId)));\n41\t    const creditMap = await getCreditBalancesForUsers(userIds);\n42\t\n43\t    // Add credits to each license object\n44\t    const licensesWithCredits = licenses.map((license) => ({\n45\t      id: license.id,\n46\t      key: license.key,\n47\t      status: license.status,\n48\t      created_at: license.createdAt.toISOString(),\n49\t      stripe_customer_id: license.stripeCustomerId,\n50\t      polar_customer_id: license.polarCustomerId,\n51\t      credits: creditMap.get(license.userId) || 0,\n52\t    }));\n53\t\n54\t    // Total credits = the sum of the DISTINCT account balances (not a per-license\n55\t    // sum, which would double-count a multi-key account's pooled balance).\n56\t    const totalCredits = userIds.reduce(\n57\t      (sum, uid) => sum + (creditMap.get(uid) || 0),\n58\t      0\n59\t    );\n60\t\n61\t    return NextResponse.json({\n62\t      user: {\n63\t        id: user.id,\n64\t        email: user.email,\n65\t      },\n66\t      licenses: licensesWithCredits,\n67\t      totalCredits,\n68\t    });\n69\t  } catch (error) {\n70\t    console.error(\"Profile API error:\", error);\n71\t    return NextResponse.json(\n72\t      { error: \"Internal server error\" },\n73\t      { status: 500 }\n74\t    );\n75\t  }\n76\t}\n77\t"}]},"uuid":"18413de6-ffa8-487f-aebc-0a8c02c98f46","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:43:15.384Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/app/api/customer/profile/route.ts","content":"import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from \"next/server\";\n\nimport { auth } from \"@/src/lib/auth\";\nimport {\n  getAccountKeysByEmail,\n  getCreditBalancesForUsers,\n} from \"@/src/lib/db-layer\";\n\n/**\n * Customer Profile API\n *\n * Returns the authenticated customer's profile and license information.\n *\n * GET /api/customer/profile\n * Returns:\n * - user: { id, email }\n * - licenses: Array of license objects with credits\n * - totalCredits: Sum of all credits across licenses\n *\n * Requires authentication via Better Auth session cookie.\n */\nexport async function GET(req: NextRequest) {\n  const session = await auth.api.getSession({ headers: req.headers });\n  if (!session?.user) {\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: \"Unauthorized\" },\n      { status: 401 }\n    );\n  }\n  const user = session.user;\n\n  try {\n    const userEmail = user.email?.toLowerCase() ?? \"\";\n\n    // Fetch user's license keys from database\n    const licenses = await getAccountKeysByEmail(userEmail);\n\n    // Credits are pooled per account. Resolve balances by the licenses' distinct\n    // owning users; each license then reports its account balance.\n    const userIds = Array.from(new Set(licenses.map((l) => l.userId)));\n    const creditMap = await getCreditBalancesForUsers(userIds);\n\n    // Add credits to each license object\n    const licensesWithCredits = licenses.map((license) => ({\n      id: license.id,\n      key: license.key,\n      status: license.status,\n      created_at: license.createdAt.toISOString(),\n      stripe_customer_id: license.stripeCustomerId,\n      polar_customer_id: license.polarCustomerId,\n      credits: creditMap.get(license.userId) || 0,\n    }));\n\n    // Total credits = the sum of the DISTINCT account balances (not a per-license\n    // sum, which would double-count a multi-key account's pooled balance).\n    const totalCredits = userIds.reduce(\n      (sum, uid) => sum + (creditMap.get(uid) || 0),\n      0\n    );\n\n    return NextResponse.json({\n      user: {\n        id: user.id,\n        email: user.email,\n      },\n      licenses: licensesWithCredits,\n      totalCredits,\n    });\n  } catch (error) {\n    console.error(\"Profile API error:\", error);\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: \"Internal server error\" },\n      { status: 500 }\n    );\n  }\n}\n","numLines":77,"startLine":1,"totalLines":77}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f77593c7-8dd1-4cc2-b7ce-366d7a996edd","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

Request comes in to /api/trpc/[procedure.name]\n12\t * 2. fetchRequestHandler parses the procedure path and input\n13\t * 3. createContext creates TRPCContext with Better Auth\n14\t * 4. Router matches procedure and executes with context\n15\t * 5. Response serialized with superjson and returned\n16\t */\n17\timport { fetchRequestHandler } from \"@trpc/server/adapters/fetch\";\n18\timport { getHTTPStatusCodeFromError } from \"@trpc/server/http\";\n19\timport { type NextRequest } from \"next/server\";\n20\t\n21\timport { appRouter } from \"@/server/api/root\";\n22\timport { createTRPCContext } from \"@/server/api/trpc\";\n23\t\n24\t/**\n25\t * Wraps createTRPCContext to handle incoming HTTP requests.\n26\t *\n27\t * @param req - Next.js request object\n28\t * @returns Promise resolving to TRPCContext\n29\t */\n30\tconst createContext = async (req: NextRequest) => {\n31\t  return createTRPCContext({\n32\t    headers: req.headers,\n33\t  });\n34\t};\n35\t\n36\t/**\n37\t * Main request handler for tRPC.\n38\t *\n39\t * @param req - Next.js App Router request\n40\t * @returns HTTP response with tRPC result\n41\t */\n42\tconst handler = (req: NextRequest) =>\n43\t  fetchRequestHandler({\n44\t    endpoint: \"/api/trpc\",\n45\t    req,\n46\t    router: appRouter,\n47\t    createContext: () => createContext(req),\n48\t    // Log server-side errors so production failures (DB outages, unexpected\n49\t    // exceptions, Stripe/Polar errors inside mutations) are visible to\n50\t    // operators and surfaced to observability sinks.\n51\t    //\n52\t    // Expected client/auth errors (invalid input, rate limits, unauthenticated\n53\t    // requests) map to 4xx status codes and are normal traffic — promoting them\n54\t    // to console.error in production would flood observability with user noise\n55\t    // and bury real 5xx incidents. So in production we only emit console.error\n56\t    // for unexpected 5xx failures; in development we log everything (plus the\n57\t    // full stack) for local debugging.\n58\t    onError: ({ path, error, type }) => {\n59\t      const isDev = process.env.NODE_ENV === \"development\";\n60\t      const httpStatus = getHTTPStatusCodeFromError(error);\n61\t      const isServerError = httpStatus >= 500;\n62\t\n63\t      if (isServerError || isDev) {\n64\t        const message = `tRPC failed on ${type} ${path ?? \"<no-path>\"}: ${error.code} - ${error.message}`;\n65\t        if (isServerError) {\n66\t          console.error(message);\n67\t        } else {\n68\t          // Expected 4xx in development — keep visible but not as an error.\n69\t          console.debug(message);\n70\t        }\n71\t      }\n72\t\n73\t      if (isDev && error.stack) {\n74\t        console.error(error.stack);\n75\t      }\n76\t    },\n77\t  });\n78\t\n79\t// Export handlers for both GET and POST methods\n80\t// tRPC uses GET for queries, POST for mutations\n81\texport { handler as GET, handler as POST };\n82\t"}]},"uuid":"8330eb8d-c769-4c0f-8c07-38933745331b","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:43:15.726Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/app/api/trpc/[trpc]/route.ts","content":"/**\n * tRPC HTTP Handler for Next.js App Router\n *\n * This catch-all route handles all tRPC requests at /api/trpc/*\n *\n * KEY DIFFERENCE FROM PAGES ROUTER:\n * - Uses fetchRequestHandler from @trpc/server/adapters/fetch\n * - NOT createNextApiHandler which is for Pages Router\n *\n * REQUEST FLOW:\n * 1. Request comes in to /api/trpc/[procedure.name]\n * 2. fetchRequestHandler parses the procedure path and input\n * 3. createContext creates TRPCContext with Better Auth\n * 4. Router matches procedure and executes with context\n * 5. Response serialized with superjson and returned\n */\nimport { fetchRequestHandler } from \"@trpc/server/adapters/fetch\";\nimport { getHTTPStatusCodeFromError } from \"@trpc/server/http\";\nimport { type NextRequest } from \"next/server\";\n\nimport { appRouter } from \"@/server/api/root\";\nimport { createTRPCContext } from \"@/server/api/trpc\";\n\n/**\n * Wraps createTRPCContext to handle incoming HTTP requests.\n *\n * @param req - Next.js request object\n * @returns Promise resolving to TRPCContext\n */\nconst createContext = async (req: NextRequest) => {\n  return createTRPCContext({\n    headers: req.headers,\n  });\n};\n\n/**\n * Main request handler for tRPC.\n *\n * @param req - Next.js App Router request\n * @returns HTTP response with tRPC result\n */\nconst handler = (req: NextRequest) =>\n  fetchRequestHandler({\n    endpoint: \"/api/trpc\",\n    req,\n    router: appRouter,\n    createContext: () => createContext(req),\n    // Log server-side errors so production failures (DB outages, unexpected\n    // exceptions, Stripe/Polar errors inside mutations) are visible to\n    // operators and surfaced to observability sinks.\n    //\n    // Expected client/auth errors (invalid input, rate limits, unauthenticated\n    // requests) map to 4xx status codes and are normal traffic — promoting them\n    // to console.error in production would flood observability with user noise\n    // and bury real 5xx incidents. So in production we only emit console.error\n    // for unexpected 5xx failures; in development we log everything (plus the\n    // full stack) for local debugging.\n    onError: ({ path, error, type }) => {\n      const isDev = process.env.NODE_ENV === \"development\";\n      const httpStatus = getHTTPStatusCodeFromError(error);\n      const isServerError = httpStatus >= 500;\n\n      if (isServerError || isDev) {\n        const message = `tRPC failed on ${type} ${path ?? \"<no-path>\"}: ${error.code} - ${error.message}`;\n        if (isServerError) {\n          console.error(message);\n        } else {\n          // Expected 4xx in development — keep visible but not as an error.\n          console.debug(message);\n        }\n      }\n\n      if (isDev && error.stack) {\n        console.error(error.stack);\n      }\n    },\n  });\n\n// Export handlers for both GET and POST methods\n// tRPC uses GET for queries, POST for mutations\nexport { handler as GET, handler as POST };\n","numLines":82,"startLine":1,"totalLines":82}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b043bef0-ffdc-4b67-8e2b-4ebce95055d5","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"abf9e37f-1bbd-4d04-8170-3812bbeae132","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"103cde84-18ec-4036-bbbf-c8f26cc8bae4","agentId":"a5d6827d8ff2888aa","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JZQATo5TdrM6kRAm72L4nU","type":"tool_result","content":"export async function getCreditBalance(userId: string): Promise<number> {\n  const [cachedRow, grantsTotal] = await Promise.all([\n    db.query.creditBalances.findFirst({\n      where: eq(creditBalances.userId, userId),\n    }),\n    getActiveGrantsTotal(db, userId),\n  ]);\n\n  const cached = cachedRow ? Number(cachedRow.balance) : null;\n  if (cached !== grantsTotal) {\n    // Drift detected (e.g. a legacy balance row with no matching grants, or a\n    // partially-applied write): heal the cache to the authoritative total.\n    await db.transaction((tx) => reconcileCreditBalance(tx, userId));\n  }\n\n  return grantsTotal;\n}\n\n/**\n * Spendable credit balances for many accounts at once (dashboard / admin list).\n *\n * Like {@link getCreditBalance}, the credit_grants rows are the source of truth\n * and credit_balances is only a cache. This reads the authoritative grant total\n * (SUM of remaining_amount across each account's still-active grants) directly,\n * so an account whose cached balance has drifted above its true grant total never\n * shows phantom credits on the dashboard. Accounts with no active grants default\n * to 0. (The cache still self-heals on the next single-account ledger\n * operation; this read path simply never relies on it.)\n */\nexport async function getCreditBalancesForUsers(userIds: string[]): Promise<Map<string, number>> {\n  const map = new Map<string, number>();\n  if (userIds.length === 0) return map;\n  // Default every requested id to 0 so accounts with no active grants are\n  // explicitly present in the map.\n  for (const id of userIds) {\n    map.set(id, 0);\n  }\n  const rows = await db\n    .select({\n      userId: creditGrants.userId,\n      total: sql<string>`COALESCE(SUM(${creditGrants.remainingAmount}), 0)`,\n    })\n    .from(creditGrants)\n    .where(\n      and(\n        inArray(creditGrants.userId, userIds),\n        eq(creditGrants.status, \"active\"),\n        sql`${creditGrants.remainingAmount} > 0`,\n        sql`(${creditGrants.expiresAt} IS NULL OR ${creditGrants.expiresAt} > now())`\n      )\n    )\n    .groupBy(creditGrants.userId);\n  for (const row of rows) {\n    map.set(row.userId, Number(row.total ?? 0));\n  }\n  return map;\n}\n\n/**\n * Attach each license's pooled account balance. Credits are pooled per account,\n * so balances are resolved by the licenses' distinct owning users and every\n * license of an account reports that one shared balance. Returns [] for empty\n * input without issuing a query.\n */\nasync function attachPooledCredits<T extends { userId: string }>(\n  licenses: T[]\n): Promise<Array<T & { credits: number }>> {\n  if (licenses.length === 0) return [];\n  const balanceMap = await getCreditBalancesForUsers(\n    Array.from(new Set(licenses.map((l) => l.userId)))\n  );\n  return licenses.map((license) => ({\n    ...license,\n    credits: balanceMap.get(license.userId) || 0,\n  }));\n}\n\nexport interface CreditGrantHistoryRow {\n  id: string;\n  userId: string;\n  createdAt: Date;\n  expiresAt: Date | null;\n  originalAmount: number;\n  remainingAmount: number;\n  status: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * Paid credit-pack grants for the given licenses, newest-first.\n *\n * Top-up history shows PAID packs only (source_type = 'stripe_credit_pack'),\n * not free/included bundles or admin/legacy grants. Expired rows are still\n * returned (their status/expiresAt let the UI show them as expired) — this is a\n * statement of purchases, distinct from the spendable-balance reads which\n * exclude expired grants.\n */\nexport async function getPaidCreditGrantsForUsers(\n  userIds: string[]\n): Promise<CreditGrantHistoryRow[]> {\n  if (userIds.length === 0) return [];\n  const rows = await db\n    .select({\n      id: creditGrants.id,\n      userId: creditGrants.userId,\n      createdAt: creditGrants.createdAt,\n      expiresAt: creditGrants.expiresAt,\n      originalAmount: creditGrants.originalAmount,\n      remainingAmount: creditGrants.remainingAmount,\n      status: creditGrants.status,\n    })\n    .from(creditGrants)\n    .where(\n      and(\n        inArray(creditGrants.userId, userIds),\n        eq(creditGrants.sourceType, \"stripe_credit_pack\")\n      )\n    )\n    .orderBy(desc(creditGrants.createdAt));\n\n  return rows.map((row) => ({\n    id: row.id,\n    userId: row.userId,\n    createdAt: row.createdAt,\n    expiresAt: row.expiresAt,\n    originalAmount: Number(row.originalAmount),\n    remainingAmount: Number(row.remainingAmount),\n    status: row.status,\n  }));\n}\n\nexport async function getAllAccountKeysWithCreditsForAdmin(limit = 1000): Promise<\n  Array<AccountKeyRow & { credits: number }>\n> {\n  const licenses = await getAllAccountKeysForAdmin(limit);\n  return attachPooledCredits(licenses);\n}\n\n/**\n * Fetch every license (with credit balance) belonging to the given users,\n * newest-first. Used by the admin list so that a customer's full license set\n * is shown even when a search only matched a subset of their licenses.\n */\nexport async function getAccountKeysWithCreditsForUserIds(\n  userIds: string[]\n): Promise<Array<AccountKeyRow & { credits: number }>> {\n  if (userIds.length === 0) return [];\n  const rows = await db.query.accountKeys.findMany({\n    where: inArray(accountKeys.userId, userIds),\n    orderBy: [desc(accountKeys.createdAt)],\n  });\n  const licenses = rows.map(drizzleAccountKeyToRow);\n  return attachPooledCredits(licenses);\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Device Validations\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport async function upsertDeviceValidation(\n  licenseKeyId: string,\n  deviceId: string,","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"135af58a-bbaf-4da8-9081-529f3f884130","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:43:22.653Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"export async function getCreditBalance(userId: string): Promise<number> {\n  const [cachedRow, grantsTotal] = await Promise.all([\n    db.query.creditBalances.findFirst({\n      where: eq(creditBalances.userId, userId),\n    }),\n    getActiveGrantsTotal(db, userId),\n  ]);\n\n  const cached = cachedRow ? Number(cachedRow.balance) : null;\n  if (cached !== grantsTotal) {\n    // Drift detected (e.g. a legacy balance row with no matching grants, or a\n    // partially-applied write): heal the cache to the authoritative total.\n    await db.transaction((tx) => reconcileCreditBalance(tx, userId));\n  }\n\n  return grantsTotal;\n}\n\n/**\n * Spendable credit balances for many accounts at once (dashboard / admin list).\n *\n * Like {@link getCreditBalance}, the credit_grants rows are the source of truth\n * and credit_balances is only a cache. This reads the authoritative grant total\n * (SUM of remaining_amount across each account's still-active grants) directly,\n * so an account whose cached balance has drifted above its true grant total never\n * shows phantom credits on the dashboard. Accounts with no active grants default\n * to 0. (The cache still self-heals on the next single-account ledger\n * operation; this read path simply never relies on it.)\n */\nexport async function getCreditBalancesForUsers(userIds: string[]): Promise<Map<string, number>> {\n  const map = new Map<string, number>();\n  if (userIds.length === 0) return map;\n  // Default every requested id to 0 so accounts with no active grants are\n  // explicitly present in the map.\n  for (const id of userIds) {\n    map.set(id, 0);\n  }\n  const rows = await db\n    .select({\n      userId: creditGrants.userId,\n      total: sql<string>`COALESCE(SUM(${creditGrants.remainingAmount}), 0)`,\n    })\n    .from(creditGrants)\n    .where(\n      and(\n        inArray(creditGrants.userId, userIds),\n        eq(creditGrants.status, \"active\"),\n        sql`${creditGrants.remainingAmount} > 0`,\n        sql`(${creditGrants.expiresAt} IS NULL OR ${creditGrants.expiresAt} > now())`\n      )\n    )\n    .groupBy(creditGrants.userId);\n  for (const row of rows) {\n    map.set(row.userId, Number(row.total ?? 0));\n  }\n  return map;\n}\n\n/**\n * Attach each license's pooled account balance. Credits are pooled per account,\n * so balances are resolved by the licenses' distinct owning users and every\n * license of an account reports that one shared balance. Returns [] for empty\n * input without issuing a query.\n */\nasync function attachPooledCredits<T extends { userId: string }>(\n  licenses: T[]\n): Promise<Array<T & { credits: number }>> {\n  if (licenses.length === 0) return [];\n  const balanceMap = await getCreditBalancesForUsers(\n    Array.from(new Set(licenses.map((l) => l.userId)))\n  );\n  return licenses.map((license) => ({\n    ...license,\n    credits: balanceMap.get(license.userId) || 0,\n  }));\n}\n\nexport interface CreditGrantHistoryRow {\n  id: string;\n  userId: string;\n  createdAt: Date;\n  expiresAt: Date | null;\n  originalAmount: number;\n  remainingAmount: number;\n  status: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * Paid credit-pack grants for the given licenses, newest-first.\n *\n * Top-up history shows PAID packs only (source_type = 'stripe_credit_pack'),\n * not free/included bundles or admin/legacy grants. Expired rows are still\n * returned (their status/expiresAt let the UI show them as expired) — this is a\n * statement of purchases, distinct from the spendable-balance reads which\n * exclude expired grants.\n */\nexport async function getPaidCreditGrantsForUsers(\n  userIds: string[]\n): Promise<CreditGrantHistoryRow[]> {\n  if (userIds.length === 0) return [];\n  const rows = await db\n    .select({\n      id: creditGrants.id,\n      userId: creditGrants.userId,\n      createdAt: creditGrants.createdAt,\n      expiresAt: creditGrants.expiresAt,\n      originalAmount: creditGrants.originalAmount,\n      remainingAmount: creditGrants.remainingAmount,\n      status: creditGrants.status,\n    })\n    .from(creditGrants)\n    .where(\n      and(\n        inArray(creditGrants.userId, userIds),\n        eq(creditGrants.sourceType, \"stripe_credit_pack\")\n      )\n    )\n    .orderBy(desc(creditGrants.createdAt));\n\n  return rows.map((row) => ({\n    id: row.id,\n    userId: row.userId,\n    createdAt: row.createdAt,\n    expiresAt: row.expiresAt,\n    originalAmount: Number(row.originalAmount),\n    remainingAmount: Number(row.remainingAmount),\n    status: row.status,\n  }));\n}\n\nexport async function getAllAccountKeysWithCreditsForAdmin(limit = 1000): Promise<\n  Array<AccountKeyRow & { credits: number }>\n> {\n  const licenses = await getAllAccountKeysForAdmin(limit);\n  return attachPooledCredits(licenses);\n}\n\n/**\n * Fetch every license (with credit balance) belonging to the given users,\n * newest-first. Used by the admin list so that a customer's full license set\n * is shown even when a search only matched a subset of their licenses.\n */\nexport async function getAccountKeysWithCreditsForUserIds(\n  userIds: string[]\n): Promise<Array<AccountKeyRow & { credits: number }>> {\n  if (userIds.length === 0) return [];\n  const rows = await db.query.accountKeys.findMany({\n    where: inArray(accountKeys.userId, userIds),\n    orderBy: [desc(accountKeys.createdAt)],\n  });\n  const licenses = rows.map(drizzleAccountKeyToRow);\n  return attachPooledCredits(licenses);\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Device Validations\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport async function upsertDeviceValidation(\n  licenseKeyId: string,\n  deviceId: string,","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"abf9e37f-1bbd-4d04-8170-3812bbeae132","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

and(...conditions) : undefined)\n    .groupBy(accountKeys.id, accountKeys.email, accountKeys.key)\n    .orderBy(sql`count(${deviceValidations.id}) desc`);\n\n  return rows.map((r) => ({ ...r, deviceCount: Number(r.deviceCount) }));\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Emails\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport async function upsertEmail(data: {\n  email: string;\n  source?: string | null;\n  ipAddress?: string | null;\n  userAgent?: string | null;\n  country?: string | null;\n}): Promise<void> {\n  await db\n    .insert(emails)\n    .values({\n      email: data.email,\n      source: data.source ?? null,\n      ipAddress: data.ipAddress ?? null,\n      userAgent: data.userAgent ?? null,\n      country: data.country ?? null,\n    })\n    .onConflictDoNothing({ target: emails.email });\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Sent emails (outbound audit log)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport async function logSentEmail(data: {\n  recipient: string;\n  emailType: string;\n  subject?: string | null;\n  providerMessageId?: string | null;\n  status: \"sent\" | \"failed\";\n  errorMessage?: string | null;\n}): Promise<void> {\n  await db.insert(sentEmails).values({\n    recipient: data.recipient,\n    emailType: data.emailType,\n    subject: data.subject ?? null,\n    providerMessageId: data.providerMessageId ?? null,\n    status: data.status,\n    errorMessage: data.errorMessage ?? null,\n  });\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Users\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport async function getOrCreateUser(\n  email: string,\n  metadata?: { name?: string; stripeCustomerId?: string; polarCustomerId?: string }\n): Promise<UserResult | null> {\n  // Check if user exists\n  const existing = await db.query.user.findFirst({\n    where: eq(user.email, email.toLowerCase().trim()),\n  });\n  if (existing) return { id: existing.id, email: existing.email };\n\n  // Create via Better Auth's user table.\n  // emailVerified stays false: this address comes from a Stripe/Polar checkout\n  // or an admin/import grant, so the mailbox owner has never proven control of\n  // it. The magic-link plugin flips emailVerified to true on the first\n  // successful sign-in, which is the only point where ownership is verified.\n  const userId = crypto.randomUUID();\n  const [newUser] = await db\n    .insert(user)\n    .values({\n      id: userId,\n      name: metadata?.name ?? email.split(\"@\")[0],\n      email: email.toLowerCase().trim(),\n      emailVerified: false,\n    })\n    .returning();\n  if (!newUser) return null;\n\n  // Also insert an account record so the user can sign in via magic link\n  await db.insert(account).values({\n    id: `magic-link-${userId}`,\n    accountId: userId,\n    providerId: \"magic-link\",\n    userId: userId,\n    createdAt: new Date(),\n    updatedAt: new Date(),\n  });\n\n  return { id: newUser.id, email: newUser.email };\n}\n\nexport async function getUserById(id: string): Promise<UserResult | null> {\n  const existing = await db.query.user.findFirst({\n    where: eq(user.id, id),\n  });\n  return existing ? { id: existing.id, email: existing.email } : null;\n}\n\nexport async function getUserByEmail(email: string): Promise<UserResult | null> {\n  const existing = await db.query.user.findFirst({\n    where: eq(user.email, email.toLowerCase().trim()),\n  });\n  return existing ? { id: existing.id, email: existing.email } : null;\n}\n\nexport async function getUsersByIds(ids: string[]): Promise<Map<string, UserResult>> {\n  if (ids.length === 0) return new Map();\n  const rows = await db.query.user.findMany({\n    where: inArray(user.id, ids),\n  });\n  const map = new Map<string, UserResult>();\n  for (const row of rows) {\n    map.set(row.id, { id: row.id, email: row.email });\n  }\n  return map;\n}\n\n/**\n * Move a customer to a new email. Updates the user's canonical email AND every\n * license_keys.email row owned by that user, atomically, so a customer is never\n * split across two emails. The caller should pre-check uniqueness; the\n * user.email UNIQUE constraint is the final guard (a colliding write throws\n * Postgres error code 23505, which the caller maps to a conflict).\n */\nexport async function updateCustomerEmail(\n  userId: string,\n  newEmail: string\n): Promise<void> {\n  const email = newEmail.toLowerCase().trim();\n  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    // Reset emailVerified: the new address has not been proven to belong to\n    // the account holder (matches the false default for admin/import-created\n    // users). The magic-link plugin re-verifies on the next sign-in.\n    await tx\n      .update(user)\n      .set({ email, emailVerified: false, updatedAt: new Date() })\n      .where(eq(user.id, userId));\n    await tx\n      .update(accountKeys)\n      .set({ email })\n      .where(eq(accountKeys.userId, userId));\n  });\n}\n","numLines":175,"startLine":850,"totalLines":1024}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5e59f959-3a26-48a7-a81c-790289c4acb8","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

Enforcement is a\n    // lazy filter (`expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > now()`) in the read/spend\n    // queries — there is no cron, so an expired row stays status='active' but is\n    // excluded from balance and spend. New paid/minted grants are stamped\n    // created_at + 365 days; see grantCreditLotInTransaction in db-layer.ts.\n    expiresAt: timestamp(\"expires_at\", { withTimezone: true }),\n    createdAt: timestamp(\"created_at\", { withTimezone: true })\n      .notNull()\n      .defaultNow(),\n    updatedAt: timestamp(\"updated_at\", { withTimezone: true })\n      .notNull()\n      .defaultNow(),\n  },\n  (table) => [\n    uniqueIndex(\"credit_grants_source_unique\").on(\n      table.sourceType,\n      table.sourceId,\n    ),\n    index(\"credit_grants_user_source_remaining_idx\").on(\n      table.userId,\n      table.sourceType,\n      table.remainingAmount,\n    ),\n    // Supports the per-account active-and-unexpired scan used by balance/spend.\n    index(\"credit_grants_user_status_expires_idx\").on(\n      table.userId,\n      table.status,\n      table.expiresAt,\n    ),\n  ],\n);\n\nexport const creditGrantsRelations = relations(creditGrants, ({ one }) => ({\n  user: one(user, {\n    fields: [creditGrants.userId],\n    references: [user.id],\n  }),\n}));\n","numLines":69,"startLine":1,"totalLines":69}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b437b779-49f3-42c2-9b49-91694247073a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}
