{"parentUuid":"eee3fcb2-5d74-46e3-a192-08c5d8a31655","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a5d6827d8ff2888aa","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdWJPRx4kZkvVvbLi41xN","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read-only trace complete. Findings below.\n\n---\n\n# T12 — email → wallet resolution (C5) + Stripe portal escalation\n\n---\n\n## BUG 1 — `admin.customers.grant` is the one writer that doesn't normalize; the reader compares case-sensitively → the granted license and its credits are invisible on the dashboard forever\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/server/api/routers/admin/customers.ts:196` `grant` — input `z.object({ email: z.string().email() })`, no normalization applied to `email`\n2. `.../customers.ts:211` `getOrCreateUser(email, {name})` → `nextjs/src/lib/db-layer.ts:938` looks up `eq(user.email, email.toLowerCase().trim())`, and `:953` **inserts the user row lowercased+trimmed**\n3. `.../customers.ts:217-222` `insertAccountKey({ key, email, userId, status })` — **line 219 passes the raw input `email`**\n4. `db-layer.ts:109-122` `insertAccountKey` does no normalization of its own (`:115 email: data.email`)\n5. `db-layer.ts:186-192` `getAccountKeysByEmail` → `:188 where: eq(accountKeys.email, email.toLowerCase())`\n6. `nextjs/src/db/schema/account-keys.ts:21` `email: text(\"email\").notNull()` — plain `text`, **not `citext`**; `idx_account_keys_email` is a plain btree, so `eq` is byte-exact\n7. Consumers: EP-43 `customer.ts:40`, EP-44 `:98`, EP-45 `:144`, EP-46 `:179`, EP-35 `:200`, EP-29 `profile/route.ts:36`\n\n**seam** — `admin/customers.ts:219` (writer) ↔ `db-layer.ts:188` (reader). The reader lowercases *the probe* and therefore silently assumes every writer lowercased *the stored value*. Four of five writers honour that; one does not:\n\n```\nlib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:99    email: customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim(),\nlib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:359   email: customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim(),\nsrc/lib/license-validation.ts:72     email: email.toLowerCase().trim(),\nsrc/lib/db-layer.ts:267              email: normalizedEmail,\nserver/api/routers/admin/customers.ts:219   email,          <-- raw\n```\n\n**trigger_sketch** — Admin opens the customers page and grants a key to a customer whose email they typed or pasted with any capitalisation: `Bob@Example.com` (extremely common — copied out of a support email's `From:` header, which preserves display casing). `getOrCreateUser` finds/creates `user.email = 'bob@example.com'`; `account_keys.email = 'Bob@Example.com'`. `grantCreditLot` (`customers.ts:229`) attaches 5000 credits to that userId, and `emailService.sendLicenseKey` delivers a working key.\n\n**consequence** — The customer's key works everywhere it is keyed by `key` (desktop validation, `POST /api/auth/sign-in/license-key`, `/api/checkout/credits` top-up). But every email-keyed surface returns nothing:\n- EP-43/EP-29: `licenses: []` → `UserDashboardClient.tsx:73` renders **\"No Account Keys found\"** *while the user is signed in with that exact key*\n- EP-44 `credits`: `totalCredits: 0` — the 5000 credits appear to have vanished\n- EP-45: `{ grants: [] }`, `CreditHistoryCard` renders nothing\n- EP-46/EP-35: `hasStripe: false`, so the Stripe Portal button never renders; if forced, `NOT_FOUND \"No Stripe billing history found\"`\n\nNote the false-negative trap for support: `searchAccountKeysByEmail` (`db-layer.ts:216`) uses `ilike(...)`, so the **admin** dashboard finds the row fine and shows the customer as healthy. Only the customer sees nothing. This is deterministic — no race, no concurrency.\n\nSecondary: it is also the only writer that can produce two `account_keys` rows for the same human that don't collide on email (`Bob@X.com` and `bob@x.com`), permanently splitting the key set as seen by every reader.\n\n**confidence** — high\n\n---\n\n## BUG 2 — tRPC `errorFormatter` is a no-op, so raw Stripe / Postgres error text is painted onto the customer's dashboard\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/server/api/trpc.ts:64-69` — `initTRPC…create({ transformer: superjson, errorFormatter({ shape }) { return shape; } })`\n2. tRPC's default `shape.message` is the *raw* `error.message`; only `data.stack` is stripped in production. A no-op formatter redacts nothing.\n3. EP-35 `customer.ts:214-217` `stripe.billingPortal.sessions.create({ customer: licenseWithStripe.stripeCustomerId, return_url })` — **not wrapped in try/catch**\n4. `app/api/trpc/[trpc]/route.ts:58-76` `onError` only *logs*; it never rewrites the shape\n5. `components/customer/dashboard/BillingCard.tsx:42-46` renders `{stripePortalMutation.error.message}` verbatim\n6. `app/[locale]/user/(authenticated)/dashboard/UserDashboardClient.tsx:54` renders `{error.message}` verbatim for EP-43\n\n**seam** — `trpc.ts:66` (the formatter that *looks* like it sanitizes but doesn't) ↔ `customer.ts:214` / `BillingCard.tsx:44`. The transport layer assumes procedures throw only curated `TRPCError`s; `stripePortalUrl` and every `db-layer` call assume something upstream sanitizes. Neither is true.\n\nCompare the REST twin: `app/api/customer/profile/route.ts:69-74` wraps everything and returns a flat `{ error: \"Internal server error\" }`. **The two twins of the same operation have opposite error contracts** — the snake_case REST version redacts, the tRPC version leaks. That divergence is itself the tell.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — Any of, all reachable by an ordinary authenticated non-admin clicking one button:\n- Stripe portal has no default configuration in the current mode → `\"No configuration provided and your test mode default configuration has not been created…\"`\n- The cached `stripeCustomerId` was deleted in Stripe (or was written from the other mode) → `\"No such customer: 'cus_XXXX'; a similar object exists in test mode, but a live mode key was used…\"` — **echoes the Stripe customer id and confirms mode confusion**\n- Connection-pool exhaustion / DB restart during EP-43 → Postgres driver text (`too many connections for role \"…\"`, `password authentication failed for user \"…\"`, host:port from the connection string) rendered into the dashboard at `UserDashboardClient.tsx:54`\n\n**consequence** — Internal infrastructure detail (Stripe customer ids, Stripe mode, DB role/host/pool state) rendered as red text on an unprivileged customer's dashboard. Also gives an attacker a probe oracle for whether a given `cus_` id exists.\n\n**confidence** — high (mechanism), medium (on the sensitivity of any single leaked string)\n\n---\n\n## BUG 3 — an `account_keys` row orphaned from its owner's email is a full account-takeover chain, because the license key is a session-minting credential and is returned in plaintext\n\n**trace**\n1. `db-layer.ts:1005-1023` `updateCustomerEmail` — one transaction: `UPDATE user SET email=… WHERE id=$1` then `UPDATE account_keys SET email=… WHERE user_id=$1`\n2. `lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:84` `getOrCreateUser(customerEmail)` → `:98-105` `insertAccountKey({ email: customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim(), userId: user.id, … })` — a **separate, non-overlapping transaction** that read `customerEmail` from the Stripe session minutes earlier\n3. `db-layer.ts:188` `getAccountKeysByEmail` resolves entitlement purely by `account_keys.email`; it never re-checks `user.email`\n4. EP-43 `customer.ts:62` returns `key: license.key` in plaintext; EP-29 `profile/route.ts:46` returns `key: license.key` in plaintext\n5. `src/lib/auth-license-key-plugin.ts:28-74` `POST /api/auth/sign-in/license-key`: `findAccountByKey(licenseKey)` → `createSession(license.userId)` → `setSessionCookie`. **No email challenge, no ownership proof, no `emailVerified` check.**\n\n**seam** — `updateCustomerEmail` (`db-layer.ts:1018-1021`) ↔ `insertAccountKey` (`db-layer.ts:109`). The rename transaction guarantees \"no `account_keys` row for user U carries a stale email\" **only over rows that exist at commit time**. `insertAccountKey` assumes the email it was handed is still that user's canonical email. Neither guarantees the other. The second seam is `getAccountKeysByEmail` ↔ `signInLicenseKey`: the reader treats the key as a display string, the auth plugin treats the same string as a bearer credential.\n\n**trigger_sketch**\n1. User A (`a@x.com`) completes a Stripe credit checkout. The webhook is in flight; it has already resolved `customerEmail = 'a@x.com'` and `user.id = A`.\n2. Concurrently, support runs `admin.customers.updateEmail({ userId: A, newEmail: 'a2@x.com' })` (`customers.ts:138`, the documented \"customer changed their email\" flow). It commits; `UPDATE account_keys … WHERE user_id = A` does not see the webhook's uncommitted insert.\n3. Webhook commits → row `account_keys(email='a@x.com', user_id=A)` exists while `user A.email = 'a2@x.com'`. **Nothing in the codebase ever reconciles this.** (`getUserByEmail`/23505 at `customers.ts:157-176` guard `user.email` only — they cannot see the orphan.)\n4. `a@x.com` is now genuinely free. Later a *different* human registers it via magic link, or buys credits with it (`getOrCreateUser` at `stripe-webhook.ts:84` creates user D).\n5. D signs in. `ctx.user.email = 'a@x.com'` → `getAccountKeysByEmail('a@x.com')` returns **A's orphaned row**.\n\n**consequence** — Escalating, all in one dashboard load:\n- EP-43 `licensesWithCredits` / EP-29 `profile` hand D **A's license key in plaintext** (`customer.ts:62`, `profile/route.ts:46`)\n- The DISTINCT-userId sum goes live for the first time: `customer.ts:55` / `:110` / `profile/route.ts:40` compute `Set([D, A])`, and `getCreditBalancesForUsers` (`db-layer.ts:669`) returns both balances → `totalCredits` at `customer.ts:72-75` is **D's balance + A's balance**. D sees and (via the key) can spend A's credits.\n- EP-45 `creditHistory` (`customer.ts:151-153`) returns **A's purchase history** — amounts and dates of another customer's Stripe purchases\n- EP-35 `stripePortalUrl` (`customer.ts:200-217`) picks `licenses.find(l => l.stripeCustomerId)` — A's — and mints a Stripe billing portal session for **A's Stripe customer**: A's invoices, receipts, billing address, card brand/last4, and mutation rights over A's payment methods\n- D POSTs the plaintext key to `/api/auth/sign-in/license-key` → session as **user A**. Total account takeover, and the log line at `auth-license-key-plugin.ts:80-82` records it as a normal sign-in for user A.\n\n**Exact condition required** (stated precisely, as asked): *at least one `account_keys` row whose `email` column lowercases to the attacker's session email but whose `user_id` is not the attacker's user id.* I verified the two obvious ways this is **closed**: (a) `user.email` is `UNIQUE` (`schema/auth.ts:8`) and Better Auth's `internalAdapter.createUser` lowercases on insert (`node_modules/better-auth/dist/db/internal-adapter.mjs:78-84`), so two `user` rows cannot differ only by case; (b) all five `insertAccountKey` call sites derive `userId` from `getOrCreateUser(<same email>)`, so the row is consistent *at write time*. The only remaining generator is post-write divergence — i.e. the rename/insert race above (or any manual DB repair). Note the same orphan is produced by a plain **webhook retry after a rename**: `handleCreditMint` at `stripe-webhook.ts:315-317` re-derives the license by `getAccountKeysByEmail(session-email)` on every delivery, so a redelivery after the rename can re-pool onto stale-email rows.\n\n**confidence** — medium (state is race-gated) / high (once the state exists, every consequence above follows deterministically from code I read)\n\n---\n\n## BUG 4 — unauthenticated `/api/checkout/credits` binds a Stripe customer to a license by *email match only*; EP-35 then opens that customer's billing portal\n\n**trace**\n1. `app/api/checkout/credits/route.ts:36` `POST` — **no auth**; only a license key is required\n2. `:113` `const email = license.email` — the license row's email, from `findAccountByKey`\n3. `:126-129` `stripe.customers.list({ email, limit: 1 })` — **`limit: 1` with no ordering and no ownership check.** Stripe emails are neither unique nor verified; `customers.list` returns most-recently-created first\n4. `:141` `await updateAccountKey(license.id, { stripeCustomerId })` — persisted permanently\n5. `:151-152` the checkout session itself is created with `customer: stripeCustomerId`\n6. EP-46 `customer.ts:181` flips `hasStripe: true` → `BillingCard.tsx:15` renders the button\n7. EP-35 `customer.ts:201` `licenses.find(l => l.stripeCustomerId)` → `:214` `billingPortal.sessions.create({ customer })`\n\n**seam** — `checkout/credits/route.ts:132` (writer: \"a Stripe customer with this email is *this* license's customer\") ↔ `customer.ts:214-215` (reader: \"the `stripeCustomerId` on this license is safe to mint a portal session for\"). The writer's guarantee is *email string equality inside Stripe's tenant*; the reader's assumption is *ownership*. Nothing bridges the two. There is no `stripe.customers.retrieve` verification, no metadata check, and no re-check at portal time.\n\n**trigger_sketch**\n1. Attacker completes one $5 mint checkout at `/credits`, supplying `email: \"victim@x.com\"` — accepted verbatim at `route.ts:72-77` and passed as `customer_email`, with `customer_creation: \"always\"` (`:154`). A Stripe customer with email `victim@x.com` now exists, created by and known to the attacker.\n2. Victim holds a **Polar-imported or admin-granted key**, so `account_keys.stripe_customer_id IS NULL` (only the Stripe paths ever set it). This is the vulnerable population and it is large.\n3. Victim tops up their credits from the dashboard (`CloudCreditsCard.tsx:60` POSTs their own key to `/api/checkout/credits`). `:125` sees the null, `:126` lists by `victim@x.com`, gets the **attacker-planted** customer (newest first), and `:141` writes it onto the victim's license row permanently.\n4. Victim's card and billing address are now saved onto a Stripe customer object seeded by the attacker's checkout. Victim clicks \"Stripe Portal\" → EP-35 opens the portal for **that** customer.\n\n**consequence** — Cross-tenant billing surface: the victim's portal lists the attacker's original invoice/receipt; the victim's own payment method is attached to a customer the attacker seeded and can continue to transact against. Symmetrically (ordinary collision, no attacker), two unrelated customers who used the same email at Stripe get merged into one billing identity, and the second one's portal shows the first one's invoices. Also: the binding is write-once and irreversible through any product surface — no code path ever clears `stripe_customer_id`.\n\nRelated, lower severity, same procedure: EP-35 `customer.ts:201` takes the **first** license with a `stripeCustomerId` under `desc(createdAt)` ordering (`db-layer.ts:189`). A multi-key account with two different `stripeCustomerId`s (one from a Polar-era row, one from a top-up) can only ever reach the newest; the older customer's invoices become permanently unreachable with no UI indication.\n\n**confidence** — medium\n\n---\n\n## BUG 5 — `creditHistory` returns `status` and the UI drops it, so refunded purchases are indistinguishable from spent ones\n\n**trace**\n1. `customer.ts:151` → `db-layer.ts:736-758` `getPaidCreditGrantsForUsers` — filters on `sourceType = 'stripe_credit_pack'` **only**; no `status` filter (deliberate, per the docstring)\n2. `customer.ts:166` `expired: g.expiresAt ? g.expiresAt.getTime() <= now : false` — derived from `expiresAt` alone, **ignoring `status`**\n3. `customer.ts:167` returns `status: g.status`\n4. `components/customer/dashboard/CreditHistoryCard.tsx:36-60` — renders `originalAmount`, `createdAt`, `expiresAt`, `expired`, `remainingAmount`. **`grant.status` is never read.**\n\n**seam** — `customer.ts:167` (server publishes the discriminator) ↔ `CreditHistoryCard.tsx` (client never consumes it). A grant clawed back by `refundCreditGrant` (`db-layer.ts:536-544`) gets `status='refunded'`, `remainingAmount='0'`, and **keeps its future `expiresAt`** — so `expired` is `false`.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — Customer buys a $10 pack, requests a refund, Stripe fires `charge.refunded`, `refundCreditGrant` runs. Dashboard now shows: `10,000 credits · <date> · expires <future date>` and `0 left`.\n\n**consequence** — A refunded purchase renders identically to a fully-spent one. The customer's own statement of purchases claims 10,000 credits were bought and consumed when they were refunded — a direct chargeback-dispute hazard, since the \"Credit Purchases\" card is the only self-serve billing record for Polar/admin-granted users (who have no Stripe portal at all).\n\nWorth noting the same reasoning **exonerates** the expiry flag: `expired` is honest. `spendCreditGrantsByProvenance` (`db-layer.ts:566`) applies the identical `ACTIVE_GRANT_EXPIRY` predicate (`:300`) that `getCreditBalancesForUsers` uses (`:688`), so expired credits are genuinely unspendable and `CreditHistoryCard.tsx:57` correctly shows `0 left` for them.\n\n**confidence** — medium\n\n---\n\n## BUG 6 — EP-29 and EP-44 have zero consumers in the repo, yet EP-29 is the widest plaintext-key surface\n\n**trace** — Repo-wide search for consumers:\n- `customer.licensesWithCredits` → `UserDashboardClient.tsx:28` ✅\n- `customer.creditHistory` → `CreditHistoryCard.tsx:13` ✅\n- `customer.billingProviders` / `customer.stripePortalUrl` → `BillingCard.tsx:6,7` ✅\n- **`customer.credits` (EP-44) → no consumer**\n- **`GET /api/customer/profile` (EP-29) → no consumer** (only its own docstring at `profile/route.ts:14`)\n\n**seam** — `profile/route.ts:44-52` (an unversioned, publicly-routable REST endpoint returning `key: license.key` in plaintext for every license) ↔ the UI layer, which no longer calls it. It is a live, auth-gated key-disclosure endpoint with **no test coverage and no UI exercise** — the exact shape that survives a refactor of `getAccountKeysByEmail` unnoticed. It already carries a distinct contract from its tRPC twin (snake_case fields, no `minutesRemaining`, no `creditsPerMinute`, generic error body), so drift between the two is invisible.\n\n**consequence** — Every bug above (1, 3, 4) reaches production through EP-29 too, and EP-29 is the one nobody will re-check. It is also the fastest scripted key-harvest surface: one `GET` with a session cookie returns all plaintext keys as JSON, no tRPC/superjson envelope to unwrap.\n\n**confidence** — low as a bug in itself, medium as a blast-radius multiplier\n\n---\n\n## Negative findings (invariants I checked and found intact)\n\n- **`credit_balances` vs `credit_grants` cannot disagree on any traced endpoint.** `getCreditBalancesForUsers` (`db-layer.ts:669-697`) queries `credit_grants` directly and never touches the cache; `getCreditBalance` (`:640`) returns `grantsTotal`, not `cached`. A repo-wide search shows `credit_balances.balance` is *never* read into a response — the only read is the drift check at `:642`. The cache is effectively write-only. EP-44 (balances) and EP-45 (grants) therefore cannot show contradictory numbers via a stale cache.\n- **`incrementCreditBalance` (`:349-367`) does not apply the expiry filter**, so the cache *can* drift above the true total as grants lapse — but since no read path consumes it, this is latent, not user-visible.\n- **The four copies of the DISTINCT-userId idiom have not drifted.** `customer.ts:55/72-75`, `customer.ts:110/113-116`, `customer.ts:151-153`, `profile/route.ts:40/56-59` are behaviourally identical (reduce vs for-loop only). The fifth copy, `admin/customers.ts:113`, uses `customer.totalCredits = l.credits` (assignment, not sum) — correct for its per-user grouping, but it means that under BUG 3's orphan-row state the **customer dashboard over-reports (sums two accounts) while the admin dashboard under-reports (groups by user)**, so support and customer see different totals for the same data. Diagnosis trap, not a second bug.\n- **`protectedProcedure` / session expiry is sound.** `isAuthed` (`trpc.ts:77-90`) only checks presence, but `auth.api.getSession` (`trpc.ts:48`) validates expiry inside Better Auth and returns `null` past `session.expiresAt` — so presence *is* validity. `isAdmin = user?.role === \"admin\"` (`:50`) reads the DB-backed `user.role` column (`schema/auth.ts:13`), not an email whitelist as the comments at `:26` and `:146` claim; that comment drift is cosmetic, the check is correct.\n- **`createTRPCContext` does not try/catch `getSession`** (`trpc.ts:48`), so a DB outage fails *closed* (500, no session) rather than open. Correct failure direction — but it is the vector for BUG 2's DB-error-text leak.\n\n---\n\n## depth_limits\n\n- I did **not** trace the deduction endpoint (`/api/license/credits`) or the webhook signature/replay handling — flagged as another agent's units. I only established that `spendCreditGrantsByProvenance` (`db-layer.ts:555-618`) applies the same expiry predicate as the balance read, which is what \"this side\" needed.\n- **Not traced but likely material and adjacent to this spine:** `handleCreditMint`'s pooling (`stripe-webhook.ts:315-341`) resolves an existing license from the **unverified** `session.customer_details.email` and then `stripe-webhook.ts:419-425` emails `license.key` — the victim's existing plaintext key — to that address. It happens to be safe *only* because the address the key is mailed to is the same address that was used to find it. That safety is incidental, not enforced, and one line change (mailing to a `metadata`- or account-derived address) would turn it into unauthenticated key disclosure. Worth a dedicated unit.\n- **Not traced:** the `refundCreditGrant` drawdown's cross-grant clawback (`db-layer.ts:501-531`), which can zero out a victim's *own* grants after a third party buys credits onto their account via email pooling. Ledger-side; belongs to the refund unit.\n- I read Better Auth 1.6.20's `internal-adapter.mjs` to settle whether two `user` rows can differ only by email case (they cannot). I did **not** audit Better Auth's magic-link token binding or rate limiting.\n- No runtime verification: read-only, no DB, no Stripe calls, no `.env` access.\n\n##
